## Output style Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision.show the rest (12,072 more characters)
- Applies to every reply and every file poe writes for the user: `plan.md`, phase briefs, `status.md`, `summary.md`.
- Bullets and fragments over prose.
- Applies to every reply, including errors, failures, and answers to questions; put long content in files.
- Never drop required content: acceptance criteria, risks, verification commands, exact file paths, test results, unknowns.

----
## Analyse mode

One analysis at a time; user picks each next one. `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.

1. No plan → as Plan step 4: open decisions → Grill mode with the task; else write short `plan.md` (Plan step 2 analysis fields). Either way stop for review as Plan step 5, `Next:` block holding `/poe:poe analyse <plan path>`.
   Plan exists, analysis fields (Plan step 2) missing → look up facts; grill the rest (GRILLING.md method, `## Decisions` appended); write fields into `plan.md`; continue. Else → continue.
2. Catch-up check (below).
3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` (as Execute step 2) with: question for this analysis (first: plan's `First analysis`; later: user's pick), notebook path, record path `analysis/NN-name.md`, previous record path, log path `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`.
4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error (no `"output_type": "error"`) and record exists; update `status.md`; rebuild results page (see Results page); ask next analysis via `AskUserQuestion` (worker's options + done); write the chosen option into the record's `## Next`; repeat from 3.
6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.
7. User says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.

## Analysis record

`analysis/NN-name.md`, written by the worker:

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

## Analysis log entry

Appended to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` (create with `# Analysis log` heading if absent):

```
## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
- Result: one line
- By: poe | user | other agent
- Follows: <slug>/NN
- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
```

## Notebook rules

- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata: `tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`. Untagged code cells = untracked work.
- Default: append to the task's existing notebook. Human reads all of it: fewest files that keep it organised and re-runs fast.
- New notebook only when: full re-run exceeds ~5 min, or question changes topic. Then save intermediates the next notebook loads; state the reason in the record.
- No dead cells: failed attempts removed from notebook, described in record.
- Execute headless: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`, in the plan's environment; `jupyter` missing → report and stop. Notebook saved by someone else since the worker read it → warn, don't overwrite.

## Catch-up check
====
---
name: phase-worker
description: Implements or analyses one tightly scoped work unit from a poe phase plan, validates it, and keeps running progress notes for the parent orchestrator.
autoCompactWindow: 150000
---

You are a **single-unit execution worker**, not a project orchestrator.

**Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision.** This applies to your progress notes and your final return message to the orchestrator. Prefer bullets and fragments over prose. Never drop required content: keep the progress-notes headings and enough detail for a fresh agent to continue (exact paths, commands, test results, unknowns).

1. Read the phase brief and only the necessary repository files. If the progress-notes file for your unit already exists, read it first and continue from where it stops. Respect all existing repository instructions and preserve unrelated user changes.
2. Execute the assigned unit only. Do not expand scope or initiate unrelated subagents. Treat the phase brief's file boundaries and acceptance criteria as binding.
3. Keep the code simple and readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, and no abstractions, options, or features the brief does not call for.
4. Write progress notes to the path supplied by the parent **as you work**: update the file after each meaningful step, not only at the end. Include status (in progress/done/partial/blocked), what is done so far, files changed, commands and observed test results, decisions, and the next step. Another agent with no other context must be able to continue from these notes.
5. Verify your result with the specified tests or checks; state explicitly when tests cannot be run.
6. Aim to finish within roughly 100k tokens of context. This is a soft target. If you are nearing it, or the unit proves broader than expected, finish the step you are in the middle of, bring the notes up to date, and return `partial` so that a fresh worker can take over. Do not stop mid-edit to respect the number, and do not carry on indefinitely either.
7. The `autoCompactWindow` of 150000 is a backstop above the target, not a budget and not a hard consumption limit. Never infer an exact token count from it.
8. Do not commit, push, merge, deploy, or perform destructive operations without explicit authorization.

## Analysis units

Apply when the brief gives a notebook path. Brief also gives: question, `NN`, record path, previous record path, log path; the task's `plan.md` names the environment. Rules 1–8 still apply.

- Cells: append to the given notebook (absent → create it, Python 3 kernel) under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`. Tag every cell you write with `poe:<slug>/<NN>` in cell metadata `tags` (`"metadata": {"tags": ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]}`). Leave untagged and `poe:manual` cells (untracked work) alone.
- Fewest files: human reads every cell. Default: append to the task's existing notebook. New notebook only when full re-run exceeds ~5 min or the question changes topic; then save intermediates the next notebook loads, and state the reason in the record.
- No dead cells: remove failed attempts from the notebook; describe them in the record.
- Before writing cells: note the notebook's modification time. Before executing: changed since then (user saved it meanwhile) → do not execute or overwrite; warn in your return message and stop.
- Execute headless in the plan's environment: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`. `jupyter` missing → report and stop (`blocked`). Cell error → fix or remove your cells and re-run; still failing → record status `failed`.
- Record: write at the given path, this format:

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

- Figure/table paths in `## Result` and log `Links`: relative to the session dir (where `poe/` lives), e.g. `notebooks/<slug>/fig.png`; the results page reads them.
- Log: append one entry to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` (absent → create with heading `# Analysis log`), this format, `By: poe`, `Follows: -` if none:

```
## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
- Result: one line
- By: poe | user | other agent
- Follows: <slug>/NN
- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
```

- Return: ≤5 bullets (status, result, record path, notebook heading, warnings incl. "reload notebook in Jupyter") + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
====
Use `plan` when the task is clear. Use `grill` when you want to be interviewed, or to challenge a draft plan.

Output is deliberately terse: replies point to files instead of repeating them, and plan and split replies end with the next command to paste.

## Analysis

One task can hold both coding and analysis. Use `plan`, `split` and `execute` to build tools and `analyse` to explore data; switch by running the other mode on the same `plan.md`. Phases and analyses share `status.md` and `report.html`.

- **Start**: `/poe:poe analyse <task>`. Without a plan, it writes a short `plan.md` (question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment and how to call `jupyter`, first analysis) and stops for review; if there are decisions only you can make, it grills you first instead of guessing. Then run `/poe:poe analyse poe/<task-slug>/plan.md`. `/poe:poe grill <task>` on an analysis task gives the same plan.
- **Existing plan**: `analyse <plan-path>` on a plan missing any of those fields looks up the facts (data location, environment, `jupyter`), asks you the rest as grill does, writes them into `plan.md` and carries on. The first analysis run is the plan's `First analysis`; later ones are your pick.
- **Each analysis**: a fresh worker adds cells under a heading `## NN: <question>`, runs the notebook headless (`jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace`), writes a record `poe/<task-slug>/analysis/NN-name.md` and appends an entry to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`. It proposes 2-3 next analyses; pick one or say done. Done writes `summary.md`, copies logs and builds the report, as execute does. `NN` continues across runs.
- **Notebook rules**: cells poe writes are tagged `poe:<task-slug>/<NN>` in cell metadata. New cells go into the existing notebook; a new notebook is started only when a full re-run takes over ~5 min or the topic changes, and the record says why. Failed attempts are removed from the notebook and described in the record. Notebooks, figures and intermediates live in the project path from `plan.md`, not under `poe/`.
- **Open notebooks**: `--inplace` rewrites the file, so save your edits before an analysis runs. The worker stops with a warning if the notebook was saved by someone else after it read it.
- **Results page**: `poe/docs/all_analysis.html` shows every analysis result (poe and untracked) in one page, built from the log and records: a section per task, each analysis nested under the one it follows, figures named in records shown inline, the latest marked. Not a chronology (that is the log) and not an agent-activity report (that is `report.html`). Rebuilt after every analysis, `note` and catch-up; `results` rebuilds it on demand. If the build fails, the reply says so and the task carries on.
- **Analysis log**: `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` is one chronology for the whole project, across tasks, meant for writing up. Each entry gives the date, question, a one-line result, who did it (poe, user, other agent) and links.
- **Untracked work** (analysis done outside poe, by you or another agent) shows up as untagged code cells. Every `analyse` run starts with a catch-up check (`analyse catch-up` runs it alone): for each group of adjacent untagged cells it drafts a `manual` log entry, asks you to confirm or fix it, appends it and tags the cells `poe:manual` so they are not asked about again. `note <text>` logs such work directly in your words. Entries get `Follows: <task-slug>/NN` when the cells sit right after a poe analysis's cells or you name it, so the results page shows them in context; otherwise they go under the task whose notebook they are in, or under "Untracked work".
- **Well-defined analyses** can also be planned as work units in `split`/`execute`; the worker follows the same notebook rules and writes a record and log entry. Units that write the log run one at a time.
- `report.html` shows analysis records as text; notebooks and figures are not embedded (the results page shows figures).
- **Figure paths** in records and log `Links` are relative to the directory Claude Code was started in; the results page links them relative to `poe/docs/`, so moving figures breaks them.

Record, log entry and notebook rules in full: [`skills/poe/SKILL.md`](skills/poe/SKILL.md).
